# PRD alignment and UX acceptance audit

Audit opened 16 October 2026. The goal remains active. A previous passing build and status fixture did not prove completion of all music and radio flows.

## Evidence rules and target

The denominator is the full set of current requirements in `REQUIREMENTS.md`, including its music, playlist, persistence, accessibility, security, and voice-preview requirements, plus R1–R12 in `RADIO_EXPERIENCE.md`. M1–M15 there clarify the existing music requirements without replacing them. Future features remain separate as defined there; existing requirements cannot be removed to improve the score.

For the requested 90% target, report two separate results:

- Engineering acceptance coverage: verified observable acceptance checks divided by all current checks in this audit. Source inspection alone establishes implementation, not a passed interaction check. A missing or contradicted result is not a pass.
- Usability: completion without help, error recovery, perceived clarity, and audible quality require observed listener sessions. Do not infer a usability percentage from internal code review or fixture checks. The [listener session protocol](LISTENER_ACCEPTANCE.md) defines ten uncoached tasks, a separate pilot, a planned five-person validation cohort and a 45-of-50 observed-task milestone with mandatory critical checks. That cohort score is separate from engineering coverage and does not replace the full requirement denominator. No session has been run.

Every critical invariant must pass regardless of percentage: no lost edits, no unrequested paid work, no credential exposure, no silent overwrite of a selected recording, no automatic retry of uncertain paid work, no duplicate advancement/replayed speech, and working keyboard access to essential controls. Completion requires all current requirements to be implemented and verified; 90% is a progress milestone, not permission to leave a critical flaw.

## Acceptance inventory

The [observable acceptance checklist](ACCEPTANCE_CHECKLIST.md) now provides 191 working checks across the full music/radio, presentation, trust and evidence contract, with all 27 STAR stories mapped to their main checks. It is the working level for individual evidence review. Its Review, Dated, Recorded, Controlled, Live and External dispositions do not count as an overall percentage. The source requirements remain authoritative, and completeness/freshness review remains required before using the inventory as the final denominator.

The seven numbered acceptance criteria in REQUIREMENTS are parent gates. Each gate includes every linked requirement and observable result; a partly verified child keeps its parent open. The27 named STAR scenarios comprise R1–R12 and M1–M15. They are traceable below, but do not replace the separate visual, architecture, credential, source-trust and release obligations. No overall percentage is calculated until those child checks have individual evidence and the complete denominator is reviewed. A count of successful fixture cases is not that denominator.

| Parent criterion | Required evidence | Current disposition |
| --- | --- | --- |

## Human Interface Guidelines applied

Apple's [onboarding guidance](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/onboarding) supports learning through use, optional brief setup, and help beside the relevant control. Apple's [Settings guidance](https://developer.apple.com/design/human-interface-guidelines/settings) supports familiar defaults and keeping configuration focused on choices people need. The implementation implications here are optional radio setup, contextual missing-prerequisite actions, preserved edits when visiting shared management, and explicit feedback after save. These are design choices informed by the guidance, not certification of Apple compliance or proof of user satisfaction.

## Round completed in this continuation

Reproduced and fixed combined-draft loss, unreachable first-use prerequisite help, profile draft leakage risk, stale save clearing, readiness/Resume gating, discarded source warnings, obsolete take claims, and hidden audition playback. Native tests used actual main/preload/renderer, actual disk storage, a local Jellyfin/RSS HTTP fixture, fake keychain values, and substituted text/voice endpoints. The fixture shortened the scheduler poll from sixty seconds to one second; production cadence was not changed. No real provider account was charged. Production build, typecheck, full lint, and whitespace checks passed after the fixes. The goal remains active for the remaining requirement-by-requirement checks above.

## Playlist uncertainty recovery

The audit reproduced creation returning `invalid` after Jellyfin accepted the POST but returned a missing ID. Response-ID validation now reports `uncertain-write`; pre-request validation is unchanged. Both playlist dialogs freeze inputs and guard form submission after an uncertain write, refresh data and offer Close and check playlists. Add-to-playlist returns to the playlist list. Name matching is not used to infer success because duplicate names are allowed. Steve reviewed the final diff without blockers. The suspected library-cache issue was withdrawn after tracing the existing connection update, which already cancels and removes all non-connection queries; no redundant cache change was kept.

## Saved audio integrity and recovery

Reproduced an empty file being accepted for selection and a truncated file being marked available. Availability and media reads now require a positive recorded byte length and exact size; unknown-length records remain unavailable without rewriting their provenance. The final claim snapshot must still report availability, otherwise it releases the claim. An adversarial service fixture removed a file after claim and before the final snapshot and verified release instead of returning it as playable.

The review also found an older missing signature match hiding a later intact replacement, causing the normal explicit Generate action to render again. The service now searches matching takes newest-first for intact audio. Same-request replay remains tied to the original artifact, and Generate another take still bypasses reuse. Boris and Nadia supported this scoped approach; Nadia reviewed the implementation and identified the final-claim race before it was fixed. Length checks detect missing/empty/truncated audio, not same-length content changes. No audio digest was invented for existing artifacts.

## Saved DJ preview alignment

The current implementation and README described neutral voice-ID samples, while the preserved per-DJ requirement called for saved delivery previews with outdated state. These now coexist explicitly. `getDJVoicePreview` reads only the DJ cache; `prepareDJVoicePreview` renders its saved voice/tags. Neutral editor endpoints retain bundled/shared samples. Both caches share publication code but keep separate identities, attempt markers and single-flight keys.

Steve and Elena accepted a mandatory discriminated preview resource rather than duplicating the audio lifecycle; the APIs, query keys, status and regenerate action remain distinct. Review caught passive-effect cancellation timing; synchronous layout guards and generation checks on audio play events now reject stale playback. The UI replay initially changed fixture data behind the query cache and timed out; the final passing check used the actual DJ editor/save flow and its invalidation, which is the user behavior under test.

## Source coverage and shared deletion

The audit reproduced HN partial item failures being omitted from successful preparation. Internal source batches now distinguish articles from coverage warnings. Usable HN/X results survive partial errors; RSS malformed title/link entries and HN invalid IDs also warn. When no candidates survive, incomplete coverage produces a source-named failure. Otherwise warnings travel with the saved script or model no-selection reason. Undated automatic entries stay ineligible and explain why they were skipped without being classified as a network failure. Coverage warnings never include raw X error bodies or credentials.

Boris and Nadia independently supported the batch result approach over discarding an entire partially successful source. Nadia preferred a separate completeness boolean; Morgan kept an explicitly named `coverageWarnings` channel, separate from freshness explanations, so classification does not parse generic warning text. Nadia reviewed and ran the final fixture without a blocker. Source deletion now lists affected profile names rather than referring only to the active setup, and malformed URL normalization gives a recovery instruction instead of a parser message. The actual-renderer bridge check passed named deletion/Cancel/confirm, failed save retaining the draft, duplicate rejection, normalized RSS/HN/X forms, zero generation calls and 960/1280/1600 widths.

## Core player acceptance replay

The current player was bundled unchanged into a temporary React page with its actual provider, browser audio elements and real silent WAV resources. The bridge substituted stream preparation and offline/format failures. Queue tests used duplicate songs with distinct occurrence keys. Natural-end tests decoded short WAV files and used the actual ended events, rather than invoking the handler directly. Converted-stream checks verified client offset requests against local WAV responses; they do not prove Jellyfin's encoder or every input codec.

Next while paused was reproduced starting music, inconsistent with Previous and queue removal. Steve and Elena independently recommended preserving pause. After challenging separate automatic/manual routing, both supported removing the shared forced-play line because automatic continuations already retain playing intent. The focused reproduction and complete transport replay passed after the change. The existing DJ-transition fixture also passed real speech completion and paused-break stop behavior. ## Forced-stop recovery evidence, 16 October 2026

The earlier publication tests seeded durable files. This round instead ran the actual storage, prepared-news and scheduler modules in separate child processes, paused each at an instrumented boundary, sent SIGKILL from the parent and verified the exit signal before starting recovery. Provider methods and Electron/keychain integration were substituted; provider calls were written to a separate fixture log before returning or pausing. Recovery used the actual reconcilePreparedNews/startNewsWorkflow path, waited for its initial tick to become idle, and ran again in a second fresh process. Every boundary retained one started assessment; neither restart added a text or voice call.

| Forced stop after | First and second restart outcome | Saved artifacts/count |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Run reservation; request ledger registration | Interrupted and paused | No recording, zero clips |
| Text dispatch; text result return | Interrupted and paused | No recording, zero clips; no repeated text dispatch |
| Script index commit | Interrupted and paused | Script retained, no recording, zero clips |
| Voice dispatch; voice result return | Interrupted and paused | Script retained, no recording, zero clips; no repeated voice dispatch |
| Audio temporary file write; sidecar temporary file write | Interrupted and paused | Uncommitted audio stays hidden; script retained, zero clips |
| Sidecar publication; audio rename | Prepared | One recovered available recording, script/provenance retained, clip count one |
| Artifact index commit; workflow settlement | Prepared | One available recording, clip count one, no duplicate settlement |

All thirteen stops passed. Saved scripts, take identity, library contents, selection and workflow history/counters remained stable across the second fresh restart. The first fixture assertions treated optional false pause state as structurally mandatory; they were corrected to compare the same boolean meaning used by the application normalizer. This did not require an application change. Completed publication recovery retained a real bundled MP3 file; this round checked availability and exact saved metadata, while earlier native audio checks cover decoding/playback.

This is process-stop evidence for the automatic service path, stronger than seeded files. It is not a full Electron crash or power-loss test: Electron/OS credential facilities were replaced, provider endpoints were never contacted, and filesystem fsync/power-failure behavior was not exercised. Text/voice responses lost before committed publication cannot be reconstructed; the expected result is an explicit interrupted/paused state with no automatic paid retry. Full manual paid-branch, live-provider and native lifecycle acceptance remains open. No production code changed in this round, so the previous build/type/lint results remain applicable; whitespace checking passed for the evidence updates.

## Listening session restoration and empty-queue settings, 16 October 2026

The actual PlayerProvider with real muted WAV audio restored queue order, a selected duplicate occurrence, position, volume, shuffle and repeat paused. Missing earlier entries preserved the current duplicate and its offset; a missing current track selected the next surviving track at zero while paused. Transient song lookup failure retained the durable queue for explicit Retry, and explicit new playback superseded a delayed restore. These checks exposed a separate gap: volume, shuffle and repeat edits with an empty queue did not save, and final-item removal wrote null, discarding the options. Actual native controls reproduced the empty-queue loss through the production renderer and real IPC/store.

The fix keeps the existing snapshot format. Shared autosaving writes an empty snapshot with normalized index/position zero and retained options. A connection-scoped readiness gate prevents initial, interval or beforeunload saves until a matching saved queue is adopted, no saved snapshot exists, or the listener explicitly plays/enqueues replacement music. Failed local-state reads and failed restoration keep the gate closed and the recoverable snapshot intact. Explicit option edits made before the startup read resolves take precedence over saved values. During failed recovery, those edits remain in memory until successful Retry or explicit replacement; this avoids erasing a queue that has not been recovered.

Steve and Elena independently proposed gated saving and retaining options in an empty snapshot. The challenge rejected writing separately from each control because it repeats persistence and ordering logic. Elena's review found that treating an account mismatch as a fresh empty session would erase the previous account's snapshot merely on opening. The corrected branch leaves saving gated until explicit Play/enqueue. Elena reviewed the final correction without a blocker. The app already remounts PlayerProvider for account identity changes.

Focused actual-player checks passed empty-queue edits, final removal, delayed local-state control edits, no writes from beforeunload during failure/loading, no interval write during a restore delayed beyond five seconds, different-account snapshot retention and explicit replacement. Native actual-main/preload/production-renderer/IPC/store checks passed volume/shuffle/repeat edits with an empty queue across a fresh app restart. Actual JellyfinService deferred-HTTP replay reproduced an older sign-in replacing a newer accepted account. ## Actual native credential encryption, 16 October 2026

The full requirement audit still distinguished substituted credential tests from actual operating-system protection. A disposable two-process Electron43.7.0 arm64 check on macOS27.2 now used the actual storage module and Electron safeStorage implementation on this Mac, without safeStorage or shared-Keychain substitution. It wrote a dummy Jellyfin connection and a dummy local Twitter-provider key. Encryption was available; stored ciphertext contained neither the dummy token/server address nor the dummy provider key. Process inspection confirmed that disposable process remained alive; it was terminated before retry. The result establishes the current machine's local encryption path. It does not establish shared OpenAI/ElevenLabs Keychain integration, unavailable-crypto behavior without substitution, other macOS versions/architectures, system privacy UI or user-level security review. No production code, dependency or unit-test framework changed for this evidence round.

An unreadable or timed-out playlist creation remains uncertain; status-only edits remain confirmed by successful headers and cancel unused bodies best-effort. That observation timeout is not evidence of a product hang; per-request deadlines and end-to-end query latency are separate. Sign-in mutations do not automatically retry. No query-retry policy changed in this round.

The corrected native album replay passes the existing read retry, final unreachable-server feedback, retained playing song/queue/session and explicit Try again restoring the album and connected status. ## Independent installation identities and failed connection saves, 16 October 2026

The formerly unexplained401 work produced a deterministic mechanism. In three fresh actual-service live rounds, a second authentication with the same random device ID issued a different token, rejected the earlier token, and kept the new token valid. Authenticating with a different device ID left the latest earlier session valid. Production had used one literal device ID for every installation, including API, artwork/media headers and conversion parameters. This reproduction establishes the collision mechanism, without claiming forensic proof for every historical401.

Boris and Nadia independently proposed a persisted random UUID and challenged initialization, concurrent publication, old credentials and storage failure. Both revised their initial fatal-startup approach after the listening-availability requirement was considered. Primary chose a validated nonsecret identity file under app user data, exclusive publication of a complete temporary file and one process-cached fallback UUID. A corrupt existing file is preserved and uses fallback rather than risking competing repairs. The fallback keeps music available, marks connections session-only, and does not retain a newly accepted token as restorable. The same identity reaches all Jellyfin request surfaces and survives ordinary account changes, sign-out and restart. Existing encrypted connection records need no schema change.

Review then exposed a concrete failed-replacement bug: deleting an older saved connection could fail after authentication had already changed the in-memory account. The service now queues exact revision-tagged snapshots and keeps the latest successfully committed runtime account. Stale queued snapshots do not write. An in-flight successful write becomes the rollback baseline for a later failure. Failure restores that baseline only while the failed revision is current, without incrementing authentication intent or canceling a newer pending login. The same boundary covers library changes and sign-out. Nadia reviewed the final implementation without an actionable blocker.

Actual storage-module/filesystem checks pass twelve simultaneous processes choosing one durable ID, cache/restart stability, independent directories,0600 permissions, temporary-file cleanup, corrupt/unreadable-file fallback and an unwritable directory. Thirteen actual-service persistence cases pass older/newer success/failure combinations, stale queued write suppression, newer pending authentication surviving rollback, exact library-choice rollback, session-only commit/rollback and sign-out ordering. The prior twelve-case actual-main/service/storage/IPC sign-out-authentication matrix also passes against the changed code across held public-info/auth/rejected-auth/body completion and pause/delete success/failure. Its disposable initialization hook was adapted to the new startup order; it does not substitute production connection logic.

Final native actual-main/preload/production-renderer/local-HTTP checks with real safeStorage pass an older readable account playing despite a corrupt identity file. A forced saved-credential deletion failure returns to that original account with its encrypted record unchanged. Explicit retry accepts the replacement as session-only, clears the old record, shows the account notice and requires ordinary sign-in after restart. The runtime identity stays the same across that account replacement and media calls. A separate valid-file native replay passes unchanged ID through sign-out, different-account sign-in and restart, with the new account restored from actual protected storage. The server is an owned local fixture and provider keys are isolated.

Live native acceptance on Jellyfin12.2.0 additionally passes two isolated installations with different persisted IDs: the first still reads its library and decodes music after the second signs in; both still read after the first process restarts; the first plays again without another sign-in. API, artwork and audio header IDs match the installation file, and a converted-audio HEAD request accepts the matching query ID with200 at an offset of five seconds. This is identity/route acceptance, not audible converted-codec proof. A legacy encrypted profile with no identity file also opens its real catalog and decodes music without reauthentication; a prior old/new/old header probe all returned200. Windows are hidden, audio is muted, protected storage is real and providers are blocked with zero requests.

Build including TypeScript, full ESLint, whitespace and the focused album/song search regressions pass. The [device-identity record](verification/2026-10-16-device-identity.json) preserves scope, corrections, final hashes and the actual pair/review outcomes. C20 makes this previously implicit multi-installation connection requirement explicit. The inventory now has186 rows:147 Review,13 Dated,6 Recorded,8 Controlled,2 Live and10 External. Earlier185-row records remain unchanged dated evidence. Other servers/OS versions, full sign-in/lifecycle variants, live playlists/providers/codecs, VoiceOver, audible quality and observed listener understanding still need acceptance; no overall percentage follows from these checks.


## Canceling an unpublished replacement connection, 16 October 2026

The next actual-main/service/storage/IPC check held a replacement sign-in inside credential storage, then confirmed sign-out while its preparation-pause save was held. Although the older sign-in returned invalid, releasing the credential write replaced both the in-memory account and saved connection. A later pause or deletion failure retained Replacement instead of Original. This contradicted the earlier M12/C09 guarantee; the prior13-case connection-persistence matrix did not cover cancellation at this publication boundary.

Boris and Nadia independently proposed a persistence barrier with compensating writes and a staged account with a publication guard. After challenging the compensation-failure and asynchronous-check/rename gaps, both supported the staged design. Encrypted temporary-file creation stays asynchronous. The service validates the candidate revision and sign-in intent, performs the final local rename or unlink and promotes the session in one synchronous turn. Sign-in, library changes and disconnect keep the previous active state until that point. Stale or failed publication leaves it untouched; already committed accounts form the baseline for later actions. A failed cleanup after confirmed publication cannot turn that account back into an uncommitted one. Library saves do not trigger account-change media revocation. Nadia reviewed the actual implementation without a concrete blocker.

The corrected actual-main check passes all three original outcomes: successful sign-out, failed pause and failed credential deletion. The older candidate remains inactive, returns invalid and never replaces the original saved record. Eighteen focused actual-service cases distinguish staged from already committed accounts, older/newer failure combinations, invalidated queued/in-flight candidates, cancellation before/after commit, pending newer auth, session-only clears, selection changes, disconnect and post-commit cleanup failure. The prior twelve actual-main auth-order cases also pass across public-info, auth, rejection and partial-body completion during held pause and deletion outcomes.

Native actual-main/preload/production-renderer/IPC/local-HTTP replay with real safeStorage additionally holds the encrypted temporary file immediately before publication. All three sign-out outcomes retain the original account and byte-identical canonical credential while the staged sign-in is rejected; the canceled temporary file is removed. Pending actions stay disabled. Both failure branches reopen a fresh process on the original account before explicit sign-out retry succeeds. The temporary request counter was corrected to sum both processes; otherwise it falsely read zero after restart. One authentication request total is verified, so cancellation/retry does not replay the old sign-in.

Final native regressions pass session-only identity fallback, failed credential clearing, explicit replacement retry and no older-account resurrection on restart. A valid-identity replay keeps decoded music advancing through a library save, then passes sign-out, replacement account and protected restart. Live Jellyfin12.2.0 replay reopens an existing protected session without login, decodes music and saves its library choice without interruption. A fresh sign-in on the second of the two existing isolated installation IDs leaves the first playing and both libraries readable through restart. API/artwork/audio identities and the converted HEAD route remain correct. Provider keys are isolated and no provider request occurs; live audio is muted.

Build including TypeScript, full ESLint, whitespace and focused album/song search regressions pass. The [connection-publication record](verification/2026-10-16-connection-publication.json) preserves before/after observations, pair decisions, current hashes and scope. C09 and C11 now have Controlled evidence for their named engineering variants on the tested snapshot. The186-row inventory is146 Review,13 Dated,5 Recorded,10 Controlled,2 Live and10 External. These dispositions are not a90% claim. This round does not prove other storage/server platforms, audible quality, live provider/playlist delivery, VoiceOver or ordinary-listener understanding. Server-side authentication can invalidate an older token before a canceled local replacement commits; normal reauthentication remains the recovery for that server behavior.

## Repeated playlist occurrences and safe entry edits, 16 October 2026

The next live test contradicted a client assumption. A playlist containing A,B,A,C returned four positions but only three distinct PlaylistItemIds. The native table reused those IDs as row and selection keys: checking the first A checked both As, and toggling the other cleared both. The check stopped before any entry mutation and cleaned its test playlist. The official Jellyfin controller also assigns PlaylistItemId from the underlying item ID and exposes ID-addressed move/remove routes; no safe occurrence-index route was established.

Boris/Nadia independently proposed and challenged separate row/mutation identities and per-row edit availability. Both rejected automatic full-list replacement because it could overwrite another client's changes. Unique valid IDs keep their existing keys; ambiguous, missing or invalid handles get compact occurrence keys for selection. Only their Move/Remove actions are disabled, with an explanation. Play, Queue, Add, Rename and uniquely identified edits remain usable. When fetched entries change, unreliable selections clear and stable selections remain. Elena reviewed this behavior and the final conditional render adjustment without a blocker. The latter replaced a lint-rejected effect update without disabling the lint rule.

The backend rereads entry handles and move bounds in the existing serialized operation before reserving a write, rejects repeated removal targets including UUID aliases, and never dispatches synthetic keys. Nadia's review found a queued account-change gap; the captured session is now checked after preflight, inside the serialized recovery queue before marker persistence, and again before dispatch. Her follow-up review found no blocker. A crash after a reservation but before HTTP can still conservatively require review, and an external client can change entries after preflight; neither is claimed to be transactional.

The final live native replay passes independent A selection, full and selected duplicate-preserving playback queues, paused Next reaching the second identical occurrence, ambiguous IPC rejection with zero HTTP writes and unchanged recovery file, uniquely identified B move/C removal, fallback-selection clearing with stable C selection retained, unchanged existing queue through edits, and fresh playback using edited order. Fourteen actual-service/recovery-coordinator cases pass unique move/removal, duplicate/missing/absent handles, UUID aliases, repeated removal IDs, bounds, failed preflight, empty removal and account changes before or while queued for reservation. These use local HTTP plus atomicWrite/Electron-path stand-ins, so they establish guard ordering rather than OS durability. ## X search unreadable result coverage, 16 October 2026

An actual-service controlled HTTP reproduction showed null/empty/wrong-shaped envelopes and malformed posts becoming no-news, object-shaped data and numeric text becoming warning-free candidates, and mixed data losing its coverage warning. ## Account-specific player options and the last queue, 16 October 2026

The native A→B→restart B→A flow preserved A's saved queue, but B's empty-player choices reset from volume0.27/shuffle on/repeat all to volume0.75/shuffle off/repeat off after reopening. The PRD now states that volume, shuffle and repeat belong to each server/account, while the app retains one last queue. Explicit Play or enqueue may replace that queue.

Main now stores validated option triples under SHA256 account keys, separately from the last queue. A connection-checked getter supplies the active account's options. Existing snapshot options seed a missing entry; an existing valid entry takes precedence. Invalid entries are discarded independently while other state survives. Full saves refresh the active account's options, reject stale foreign owners and preserve a foreign saved queue when the current player is empty. Option-only saves can persist without an own queue and update snapshot options only for the matching owner. Clearing a snapshot likewise requires matching ownership. These guards run inside serialized mutations. The renderer loads account options alongside state and becomes ready after declining another account's queue. Its provider is keyed by connection ID, so account switches reset local edit flags.

The actual native UI flow now retains B's choices across quit/reopen without any B song-restore lookup, preserves A's exact A,B,A,C queue/index2/position12/options through B browsing and edits, and restores A paused after signing back in. Three native startup variants verify legacy migration, valid map precedence and malformed-entry isolation while retaining a valid second account entry and unrelated state. Twenty-one native IPC cases cover validation, serialized latest-queue merging, stale full saves, foreign/absent queue preservation and active-account getter/patch guards. The old record's absent/foreign-snapshot safety no-op semantics are superseded: an active account can now save its own options while preserving that queue.

## Retained DJ speech after rewind, 16 October 2026

Twelve current native retained/replacement cases passed in both transition styles, but four added active-speech rewind cases exposed a real contract mismatch. After the DJ began speaking at an ended-song boundary, seeking to10 seconds left its voice playing over the rewound music. This happened both while playing and after pausing then pressing Play. The PRD said the retained break should wait for the ending again, while the old settings footnote instead promised that speech would keep playing during a seek. The behavioral PRD governs the implemented result; the settings copy is now aligned explicitly.

Seek now pauses the same voice element at its current position, restores the music level and returns a non-tail placement to waiting. Play after a paused rewind resumes music first. At the next suitable ending, only the unheard remainder resumes. Transition budgeting uses that remaining speech duration. Retained end ownership stays intact for over-music natural endings, and completed speech stays completed in its tail without replay. With Keep the DJ off, seeking still cancels placement and preserves playing/paused intent. Explicit song or duplicate-occurrence replacement cancels the old audio placement, while an unheard automatic take may wait for another ending.

Each plan now invalidates outstanding voice-start promises with a small attempt counter. Old initial/resume success or failure cannot alter the new waiting state. An unexpected late play event actively pauses audio when the plan is not speaking or playback is not intended, before it can mark the take heard. The existing one-shot heard marker and native Audio position remain authoritative; no extra speech-started or resume-phase state was added. The DJ history says Waiting for the song ending instead of On air during deferred speech, and the finished-voice notice says Your DJ has finished.

The final40-case native suite passes on actual main/preload/production renderer, IPC/storage, protected dummy localhost accounts, real music WAV and an existing saved MP3. It includes12 retained/replacement cases,4 active rewind/resume cases,2 real near-end natural-ending cases,12 pending initial/resume resolve/reject/deferred-invocation cases and10 fading/lead-in/tail/setting-off cases. Both styles are covered. The pending-start cases preserve durable delivery state and the original heard timestamp. The native race adapter either holds a real play result or deliberately invokes native play only after rewind; these are controlled fault cases, not claims about every platform race. Selection and preparation remain unchanged in the retained/replacement flows, and the isolated profiles have no provider keys or paid calls.

Three status-layout cases pass at1280×800/default,960×640/default and960×640/200% zoom. The deferred-history status fits its scrolling popover without horizontal overflow, Escape returns focus to the DJ button, and native music volume returns to its saved0.42 level. ## Profile drafts, atomic save recovery and truthful status, 16 October 2026

The native profile journey retains sources, host, mood, interests, cadence, importance and daily recording cap across music navigation and source/DJ/key management. ## Evidence provenance audit, 17 October 2026

The [provenance guide](EVIDENCE_PROVENANCE.md) and [dated index](verification/2026-10-17-evidence-provenance-audit.json) inspect all 90 prior JSON verification records on main 35c39c6. Every record parses, is linked from the acceptance entry points, and includes a scope boundary. The index preserves each exact file hash and points to its origin and limitation fields. 70 records are controlled, 17 mix actual Jellyfin work with other controlled/native checks, 1 mixes public-source collection with controlled edge cases, 1 inspects architecture/artifacts and 1 preserves partial OS observations. Mixed labels never imply the whole record is live.

The review distinguishes muted decode from audible quality, bundled/substituted speech from live generated delivery, technical engineering journeys from observed listener sessions, registered callbacks from physical media keys, Date/scheduler adapters from sleep/wake, synthetic blur from app switching, and app-content captures from desktop context.
